        Question: How does Newton define 'quantity of motion' and how is it distinct from 'force'?

        Original Answer: Newton defines quantity of motion as the product of a body's mass and its velocity; force is that which changes this quantity—either resisting or impressing motion.

        Question: According to Newton, what are the three laws of motion and how do they apply in celestial mechanics?

        Original Answer: 1) A body remains at rest or in uniform motion unless acted on by an impressed force. 2) The change of motion is proportional to the impressed force and occurs in its direction. 3) For every action, there is an equal and opposite reaction. In celestial mechanics, these laws explain orbital persistence, gravitational acceleration, and mutual perturbations among bodies.

        Question: How does Newton derive Kepler's Second Law (equal areas in equal times) from his laws of motion and gravitation?

        Original Answer: He shows that a body under a central (centripetal) force sweeps out equal areas in equal times because the force always acts along the radius, altering direction but preserving area–time proportionality.

        Question: If an insured person takes treatment for arthritis at home because no hospital beds are available, under what circumstances would these expenses NOT be covered, even if a doctor declares the treatment was medically required?

        Question: A claim was lodged for expenses on a prosthetic device after a hip replacement surgery. The hospital bill also includes the cost of a walker and a lumbar belt post-discharge. Which items are payable?

        Original Answer: The implanted prosthetic device is payable; external aids such as a walker or lumbar belt are excluded.

        Question: If an insured undergoes Intra Operative Neuro Monitoring (IONM) during brain surgery, and also needs ICU care in a city over 1 million population, how are the respective expenses limited according to modern treatments, critical care definition, and policy schedule?

        Original Answer: IONM is covered up to its sub-limit under Modern Treatments; ICU charges are capped at 4% of the Sum Insured per day and require a hospital with ≥15 beds in cities over 1 million.

        Question: A policyholder requests to add their newly-adopted child as a dependent. The child is 3 years old. What is the process and under what circumstances may the insurer refuse cover for the child, referencing eligibility and addition/deletion clauses?

        Question: How does Newton define 'quantity of motion' and how is it distinct from 'force'?

        Original Answer: Newton defines quantity of motion as the product of a body's mass and its velocity; force is that which changes this quantity—either resisting or impressing motion.

        Question: According to Newton, what are the three laws of motion and how do they apply in celestial mechanics?

        Original Answer: 1) A body remains at rest or in uniform motion unless acted on by an impressed force. 2) The change of motion is proportional to the impressed force and occurs in its direction. 3) For every action, there is an equal and opposite reaction. In celestial mechanics, these laws explain orbital persistence, gravitational acceleration, and mutual perturbations among bodies.

        Question: How does Newton derive Kepler's Second Law (equal areas in equal times) from his laws of motion and gravitation?

        Original Answer: He shows that a body under a central (centripetal) force sweeps out equal areas in equal times because the force always acts along the radius, altering direction but preserving area–time proportionality.

        Question: If an insured person takes treatment for arthritis at home because no hospital beds are available, under what circumstances would these expenses NOT be covered, even if a doctor declares the treatment was medically required?

        Original Answer: Home treatment for arthritis is excluded under domiciliary hospitalisation for arthritis, gout and rheumatism.

        Question: A claim was lodged for expenses on a prosthetic device after a hip replacement surgery. The hospital bill also includes the cost of a walker and a lumbar belt post-discharge. Which items are payable?

        Original Answer: The implanted prosthetic device is payable; external aids such as a walker or lumbar belt are excluded.

        Question: An insured's child (a dependent above 18 but under 26, unemployed and unmarried) requires dental surgery after an accident. What is the claim admissibility, considering both eligibility and dental exclusions, and what is the process for this specific scenario?

        Original Answer: Accident-related dental surgery requiring ≥24 hours’ hospitalisation is covered; submit an in-patient claim with the dentist’s prescription and discharge summary.

        Question: If an insured undergoes Intra Operative Neuro Monitoring (IONM) during brain surgery, and also needs ICU care in a city over 1 million population, how are the respective expenses limited according to modern treatments, critical care definition, and policy schedule?

        Original Answer: IONM is covered up to its sub-limit under Modern Treatments; ICU charges are capped at 4% of the Sum Insured per day and require a hospital with ≥15 beds in cities over 1 million.

        Question: A policyholder requests to add their newly-adopted child as a dependent. The child is 3 years old. What is the process and under what circumstances may the insurer refuse cover for the child, referencing eligibility and addition/deletion clauses?

        Original Answer: Notify insurer/TPA within 30 days of adoption with proof and prorated premium; coverage may be refused if relationship proof is inadequate or deposit insufficient.

        Question: If a person is hospitalised for a day care cataract procedure and after two weeks develops complications requiring 5 days of inpatient care in a non-network hospital, describe the claim process for both events, referencing claim notification timelines and document requirements.

        Original Answer: Day-care cataract is covered with pre-authorization; for the 5-day non-network stay, file reimbursement within 15 days of discharge with full in-patient documentation.

        Question: An insured mother with cover opted for maternity is admitted for a complicated C-section but sadly, the newborn expires within 24 hours requiring separate intensive care. What is the claim eligibility for the newborn's treatment expenses, referencing definitions, exclusions, and newborn cover terms?

        Original Answer: The newborn is covered from Day 1 under the floater and eligible for separate intensive-care charges despite expiry within 24 hours.

        Question: If a policyholder files a claim for inpatient psychiatric treatment, attaching as supporting documents a prescription from a general practitioner and a discharge summary certified by a registered Clinical Psychologist, is this sufficient? Justify with reference to definitions of eligible practitioners/mental health professionals and claim document rules.

        Original Answer: Not sufficient: mental-illness cover wasn’t opted, and only psychiatrists count as eligible practitioners, not GPs or psychologists alone.

        Question: A patient receives oral chemotherapy in a network hospital and requests reimbursement for ECG electrodes and gloves used during each session. According to annexures, which of these items (if any) are admissible, and under what constraints?

        Original Answer: Oral chemotherapy sessions are reimbursable; ECG electrodes and gloves are not covered under associated consumables.

        Question: A hospitalized insured person develops an infection requiring post-hospitalization diagnostics and pharmacy expenses 20 days after discharge. Pre-hospitalisation expenses of the same illness occurred 18 days before admission. Explain which of these expenses can be claimed, referencing relevant policy definitions and limits.

        Original Answer: Pre-hospitalisation (up to 30 days) and post-hospitalisation (up to 60 days) expenses for the same illness are admissible once the inpatient claim is accepted.

        Question: If a dependent child turns 27 during the policy period but the premium was paid at the beginning of the coverage year, how long does their coverage continue, and when is it terminated with respect to eligibility and deletion protocols?

        Original Answer: Coverage continues until policy expiry date; termination and deletion are processed at renewal when the dependent is over age limit.

        Question: A procedure was conducted in a hospital where the insured opted for a single private room costing more than the allowed room rent limit. Diagnostic and specialist fees are billed separately. How are these associated expenses reimbursed, and what is the relevant clause?

        Original Answer: Room-rent costs are proportionately adjusted to the limit; separately billed diagnostics and specialist fees are reimbursed in full.
